Hi... um, yeah. It's nice to meet you too. I'm just here because, well, work has been really hard lately. I'm a hairstylist, and I love doing color, especially balayage—it's like painting, you know? I used to could do a full head of foils in like forty-five minutes. My instructor in cosmetology school said I had the fastest hands she'd ever seen. She had this really loud laugh, like a honk almost... anyway, lately, my hands just feel like they’re moving through glue. Even just sectioning the hair or mixing the color in the bowl, it takes me forever now. My manager pulled me aside yesterday—she wears these huge hoop earrings that clink when she talks—and she said people have started noticing I’m just way slower than I used to be. I don't know why I can't just move normally anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I haven't noticed them shaking or anything when I'm just sitting there. It's more just this heavy feeling, like they're weighed down. Which is weird because I used to have such steady hands. You need really steady hands to do eyeliner too, which I used to love doing before going out. My friend Jess and I would spend hours getting ready, trying all these different makeup tutorials we'd find online. She's the one who actually told me I should go to cosmetology school. We were sitting in her bedroom—she had this bright pink comforter that I always told her was way too loud—and she just looked at me and said, "You're too good at this to not do it for a living." my bookings and messing with my hours on purpose, because the slow days seem to happen right after I talk about saving money.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: Sometimes when I’m sweeping up or rinsing bowls, I hear a woman’s voice near the shampoo stations saying I’m ruining everything, even when the salon is empty or nobody is close by.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I used to chat with clients, help the newer stylists, and go hiking with friends, but now I mostly keep my head down and avoid people because I just don’t have that spark in me.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest, like there’s no point in trying to build the life I was planning.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I’ve been skipping meals without really meaning to, then realizing at night I only had coffee and a few bites of something, and my clothes have gotten looser.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I’m a burden on everyone and that I deserve what’s happening because I couldn’t keep my money, my job, or even basic salon responsibilities together.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I stare at color formulas or the appointment screen and my brain just blanks, so I second-guess simple things I used to do automatically.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but I get thoughts like it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up and nobody had to deal with me anymore.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the sadness got heavy and stayed, the voice at the shampoo stations and the belief that someone was sabotaging my bookings were happening at the same time, not separately.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, I have spent most of this whole decline feeling depressed and weighed down, with only shorter stretches where the sadness was not as prominent.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: Before the depression really took over, there was a stretch of several weeks when I was still getting up and functioning more normally, but I was already hearing the voice and feeling sure someone at the salon was interfering with my schedule.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have lost trust from my manager and clients because I make mistakes with basic salon routines, work too slowly, and cancel things I used to reliably handle. I have also pulled away from friends and stopped showing up for volunteer haircuts at the senior center.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 8 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about a year and eight months, starting after my hours were cut and my savings began draining away.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 4 months\n  manifestation: The voices and the feeling that someone is targeting my bookings have been happening for about a year and four months.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The heavy depression has been with me for about a year and two months now.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 21 / hairdresser_hairstylist_or_cosmetologist / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S028\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi... um, yeah. It's nice to meet you too. I'm just here because, well, work has been really hard lately. I'm a hairstylist, and I love doing color, especially balayage—it's like painting, you know? I used to could do a full head of foils in like forty-five minutes. My instructor in cosmetology school said I had the fastest hands she'd ever seen. She had this really loud laugh, like a honk almost... anyway, lately, my hands just feel like they’re moving through glue. Even just sectioning the hair or mixing the color in the bowl, it takes me forever now. My manager pulled me aside yesterday—she wears these huge hoop earrings that clink when she talks—and she said people have started noticing I’m just way slower than I used to be. I don't know why I can't just move normally anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a tremor in your hands when they’re resting?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed them shaking or anything when I'm just sitting there. It's more just this heavy feeling, like they're weighed down. Which is weird because I used to have such steady hands. My friend Jess and I would spend hours getting ready, trying all these different makeup tutorials we'd find online. She's the one who actually told me I should go to cosmetology school. We were sitting in her bedroom—she had this bright pink comforter that I always told her was way too loud—and she just looked at me and said, \"You're too good at this to not do it for a living.\" I miss hanging out with her. We haven't really talked much lately. I don't know, it's just... no, no shaking. Just the slowness.\n\nDoctor: Have your muscles been feeling stiff?\n\nPatient: Stiff? Um... no, not really. The instructor there, her name was Kenzie, she always wore these neon green leggings that were so bright they kind of hurt your eyes to look at in the studio mirrors. She made us do all these weird core exercises on those sliding machine things, the reformers? It was so hard, I was sore for like a week straight after the first time. Antidepressant? Um, no. I'm not taking anything like that. I don't really like taking pills anyway. Even when I get a headache, I try to just drink water or something. Um, six months? No, it's been longer than that. The voices and the feeling that someone is targeting my bookings have been happening for about a year and four months now. It started right around the time Brenda decided to repaint the salon. She chose this color called "Seafoam Whisper" or something like that. It's this really pale, minty green. Honestly, it looks a little bit like toothpaste, but she was so excited about it. She hired these painters who came in over the weekend, and they covered everything in plastic tarps. I remember because one of the tarps got stuck to my favorite styling chair and left this weird sticky residue on the armrest. I had to use rubbing alcohol to get it off, and it took forever. Rubbing alcohol smells so strong, it always gives me a bit of a headache if I use too much of it. My mom used to use it all the time when I was a kid to clean my scraped knees when I fell off my bike. I had this bright pink bicycle with streamers on the handlebars. I loved that bike. You need really steady hands to do eyeliner too, which I used to love doing before going out. My friend Jess and I would spend hours getting ready, trying all these different makeup tutorials we'd find online. She's the one who actually told me I should go to cosmetology school. We were sitting in her bedroom—she had this bright pink comforter that I always told her was way too loud—and she just looked at me and said, \"You're too good at this to not do it for a living.\" I miss hanging out with her. We haven't really talked much lately. I don't know, it's just... no, no shaking. Just the slowness.\n\nDoctor: Have your muscles been feeling stiff?\n\nPatient: Stiff? Um... no, not really. They don't feel tight or anything. I don't feel like I need to stretch them out. I used to stretch all the time when I was taking this Pilates class, though. My mom actually bought me a package of classes for my birthday last year because she said I needed to work on my posture since I'm always hunched over the shampoo bowl at work. The instructor there, her name was Kenzie, she always wore these neon green leggings that were so bright they kind of hurt your eyes to look at in the studio mirrors. She made us do all these weird core exercises on those sliding machine things, the reformers? It was so hard, I was sore for like a week straight after the first time. But um... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. No, my muscles aren't stiff right now. Just really heavy and slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you started taking an antidepressant recently?\n\nPatient: Antidepressant? Um, no. I'm not taking anything like that. I don't really like taking pills anyway. Even when I get a headache, I try to just drink water or something. My grandma used to have this whole cabinet full of orange pill bottles. It took up an entire shelf in her kitchen, right next to where she kept her fancy teacups. She collected teacups from all over the world, even though she never left the state. She had this one from London with a little gold rim that she said was her favorite, but she never actually drank out of it. She just liked looking at it. I think she had a pill for almost everything, though... blood pressure, cholesterol, vitamins. But no, I haven't started taking any new medications or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I have. Most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest. It just sits there, making me feel like there's no point in trying to build the life I was planning, you know? Like, what's the point of finishing my hours for my license? Which sucks because I've already put in so much time at the salon. I started sweeping hair there when I was 16. My boss, Brenda, she's been cutting hair since the 80s and she always tells these crazy stories about the big perms she used to do. She said they used so much hairspray back then that the floor would literally be sticky by the end of the day. She has this framed photo of herself from 1987 with hair that's teased so high it barely fits in the picture. It's actually really funny. We used to laugh about it a lot in the breakroom. But um... yeah, I just feel really sad and flat lately. It's hard to care about any of that stuff right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually high or energized?\n\nPatient: High or energized? Um... no. I don't think so. I've never really felt like that. I'm usually pretty low-key, honestly. Even when I was a kid, my mom said I was always the quiet one at birthday parties. Speaking of birthday parties, my cousin just had a huge one for her 21st last weekend. She rented out this whole rooftop bar downtown, the one with the string lights and the little fire pits? It looked so pretty in the pictures she posted on Instagram. She wore this sparkly silver dress that she ordered online, but she had to get it altered because it was way too long. The seamstress she went to actually works in the same strip mall as my salon. Her name is Maria and she always brings us these amazing homemade tamales around the holidays. They're so good, especially the green chile ones. She wraps them in foil and keeps them in this little cooler so they stay warm. But um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right. Energized. No, I've never really had anything like that. Just the heavy, sad feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices that other people can’t hear?\n\nPatient: Voices? Um... yeah, actually. It's kind of weird. Sometimes when I'm at work, like when I'm sweeping up hair or rinsing out the color bowls, I'll hear this woman's voice. She's usually over by the shampoo stations, and she just keeps saying that I'm ruining everything. It happens even when the salon is completely empty or nobody is anywhere near me. It's so unsettling. We have these really nice shampoo bowls, though. Brenda got them imported from Italy a few years ago. They have this special neck rest that's supposed to be super ergonomic so people don't get that awful kink in their neck when they're getting their color washed out. You know how sometimes you go to a salon and your neck hurts for like two days after? Yeah, Brenda hated that, so she spent a fortune on these fancy Italian ones. They're black ceramic and they look really sleek. She even bought special microfiber towels to go with them because she read online that regular cotton towels cause too much friction and frizz on wet hair. She's so particular about that kind of stuff. But yeah... it's just this woman's voice telling me I'm ruining things, even when no one is there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that woman’s voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. Actually, yeah. Before the depression really took over, like, before I started feeling so heavy all the time, there was a stretch of several weeks where I was still getting up and functioning pretty normally. I was going to work, doing my hours, but I was already hearing her voice. And I was also just... really sure that someone at the salon was messing with my schedule. Like, deleting my appointments or moving them around in the computer. We use this booking software called Vagaro, and it's supposed to be really secure, but honestly, anyone who knows Brenda's iPad password can get in. And Brenda's password is just her dog's name and her birth year. Her dog is this tiny little Pomeranian named Muffin. Muffin is so cute, she brings him into the salon sometimes and he just sits in this little pink velvet bed under the reception desk. He has this sparkly collar with a little silver bone on it. Sometimes clients bring him treats, but Brenda is really strict about what he eats because he has a sensitive stomach. She buys this special grain-free salmon kibble that costs like eighty dollars a bag. It smells terrible, honestly, like fish food, but Muffin loves it. But um... yeah, so I was hearing the voice and feeling weird about my schedule even before I got so sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Talking less? Yeah... yeah, I definitely have been. I used to be really chatty, you know? Like, I'd always be talking with my clients, asking about their kids or their vacations, and I used to help the newer stylists a lot when they were trying to figure out color formulations. And I used to go hiking with my friends all the time on the weekends. There's this really great trail up near the reservoir that we'd always go to. It's so pretty in the fall when the leaves change. We'd always pack these little picnic lunches. My friend Sarah makes the best pasta salad for those trips. She uses those little tri-color rotini noodles and puts in fresh mozzarella balls and cherry tomatoes from her mom's garden. Her mom has this huge garden in her backyard, she grows everything. Zucchini, bell peppers, even eggplants. Last summer she had so many tomatoes she started canning her own marinara sauce and giving it out in these cute little mason jars with gingham ribbons tied around the top. I still have one sitting in my pantry actually. But um... what was I saying? Oh, right. Talking less. Yeah, now I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me anymore to be social.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that someone was messing with your schedule been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nPatient: Um, six months? No, it's been longer than that. The voices and the feeling that someone is targeting my bookings have been happening for about a year and four months now. It started right around the time Brenda decided to repaint the salon. She chose this color called \"Seafoam Whisper\" or something like that. It's this really pale, minty green. Honestly, it looks a little bit like toothpaste, but she was so excited about it. She hired these painters who came in over the weekend, and they covered everything in plastic tarps. I remember because one of the tarps got stuck to my favorite styling chair and left this weird sticky residue on the armrest. I had to use rubbing alcohol to get it off, and it took forever. Rubbing alcohol smells so strong, it always gives me a bit of a headache if I use too much of it. My mom used to use it all the time when I was a kid to clean my scraped knees when I fell off my bike. I had this bright pink bicycle with streamers on the handlebars. I loved that bike. But yeah, the voice and the schedule stuff... it's been about a year and four months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
My face? Um... yeah, I guess so. It kind of goes back to what I was just saying a minute ago, you know? About how I used to chat with all my clients and help the newer stylists and go hiking, but now I just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me to even fake a smile or react to things anymore. 

Brenda actually asked me if I was mad at her the other day because I was just staring blankly at the mirror while I was sweeping up hair. The mirrors in our salon are huge, they take up literally the entire wall. We have to clean them with this special glass cleaner that comes in an aerosol foam spray, otherwise they get streaks. I hate streaks on mirrors. It reminds me of when I tried to clean my car windows with regular paper towels and it just left little white fuzzies everywhere. My car is this older Honda Civic, and the AC barely works, so I have to roll the windows down in the summer. The wind always messes up my hair, which is so annoying when you work at a salon and you're supposed to look put together. We're supposed to wear all black, too. It's supposed to look chic, but it gets so hot in the summer, especially with the blow dryers running all day. 

But um... yeah. Like I said earlier, I just don't have the energy to be social or expressive, so my face just stays pretty flat.

The seamstress she went to actually works in the same strip mall as my salon. Her name is Maria and she always brings us these amazing homemade tamales around the holidays. No, I've never really had anything like that. Just the heavy, sad feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices that other people can’t hear?\n\nPatient: Voices? Um... yeah, actually. It's kind of weird. Sometimes when I'm at work, like when I'm sweeping up hair or rinsing out the color bowls, I'll hear this woman's voice. She's usually over by the shampoo stations, and she just keeps saying that I'm ruining everything. It happens even when the salon is completely empty or nobody is anywhere near me. It's so unsettling. We have these really nice shampoo bowls, though. Brenda got them imported from Italy a few years ago. They have this special neck rest that's supposed to be super ergonomic so people don't get that awful kink in their neck when they're getting their color washed out. They're black ceramic and they look really sleek. She even bought special microfiber towels to go with them because she read online that regular cotton towels cause too much friction and frizz on wet hair. She's so particular about that kind of stuff. But yeah... it's just this woman's voice telling me I'm ruining things, even when no one is there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that woman’s voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. Actually, yeah. Before the depression really took over, like, before I started feeling so heavy all the time, there was a stretch of several weeks where I was still getting up and functioning pretty normally. I was going to work, doing my hours, but I was already hearing her voice. And I was also just... really sure that someone at the salon was messing with my schedule. Like, deleting my appointments or moving them around in the computer. We use this booking software called Vagaro, and it's supposed to be really secure, but honestly, anyone who knows Brenda's iPad password can get in. And Brenda's password is just her dog's name and her birth year. Her dog is this tiny little Pomeranian named Muffin. Muffin is so cute, she brings him into the salon sometimes and he just sits in this little pink velvet bed under the reception desk. He has this sparkly collar with a little silver bone on it. Sometimes clients bring him treats, but Brenda is really strict about what he eats because he has a sensitive stomach. She buys this special grain-free salmon kibble that costs like eighty dollars a bag. It smells terrible, honestly, like fish food, but Muffin loves it. But um... yeah, so I was hearing the voice and feeling weird about my schedule even before I got so sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Talking less? We'd always pack these little picnic lunches. My friend Sarah makes the best pasta salad for those trips. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, a period without feeling depressed? Yeah, actually. Before the depression really took over and I just felt completely drained, there was a stretch of several weeks where I wasn't feeling down at all. I was still getting up, going to work, and functioning pretty normally, but I was already hearing that voice and feeling absolutely sure that someone at the salon was messing with my schedule. Black is supposed to be slimming, but it just shows every single piece of lint and stray hair. I have this lint roller in my station drawer that I must use ten times a day. The sticky sheets always tear weirdly when you try to peel them off, though... But yeah, there were definitely a few weeks where I wasn't depressed yet, but the voice and the schedule stuff were already happening.

But um... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. No, my muscles aren't stiff right now. Just really heavy and slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you started taking an antidepressant recently?\n\nPatient: Antidepressant? Um, no. I'm not taking anything like that. I don't really like taking pills anyway. Even when I get a headache, I try to just drink water or something. My grandma used to have this whole cabinet full of orange pill bottles. It took up an entire shelf in her kitchen, right next to where she kept her fancy teacups. She collected teacups from all over the world, even though she never left the state. She had this one from London with a little gold rim that she said was her favorite, but she never actually drank out of it. She just liked looking at it. I think she had a pill for almost everything, though... blood pressure, cholesterol, vitamins. But no, I haven't started taking any new medications or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I have. Most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest. It just sits there, making me feel like there's no point in trying to build the life I was planning, you know? Like, what's the point of finishing my hours for my license? Which sucks because I've already put in so much time at the salon. I started sweeping hair there when I was 16. My boss, Brenda, she's been cutting hair since the 80s and she always tells these crazy stories about the big perms she used to do. She said they used so much hairspray back then that the floor would literally be sticky by the end of the day. She has this framed photo of herself from 1987 with hair that's teased so high it barely fits in the picture. It's actually really funny. We used to laugh about it a lot in the breakroom. But um... yeah, I just feel really sad and flat lately. It's hard to care about any of that stuff right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually high or energized?\n\nPatient: High or energized? Um... no. I don't think so. I've never really felt like that. I'm usually pretty low-key, honestly. Even when I was a kid, my mom said I was always the quiet one at birthday parties. Speaking of birthday parties, my cousin just had a huge one for her 21st last weekend. She rented out this whole rooftop bar downtown, the one with the string lights and the little fire pits? It looked so pretty in the pictures she posted on Instagram. She wore this sparkly silver dress that she ordered online, but she had to get it altered because it was way too long. The seamstress she went to actually works in the same strip mall as my salon. Her name is Maria and she always brings us these amazing homemade tamales around the holidays. They're so good, especially the green chile ones. She wraps them in foil and keeps them in this little cooler so they stay warm. But um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right. Energized. No, I've never really had anything like that. Just the heavy, sad feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices that other people can’t hear?\n\nPatient: Voices? Um... yeah, actually. It's kind of weird. Sometimes when I'm at work, like when I'm sweeping up hair or rinsing out the color bowls, I'll hear this woman's voice. She's usually over by the shampoo stations, and she just keeps saying that I'm ruining everything. It happens even when the salon is completely empty or nobody is anywhere near me. It's so unsettling. We have these really nice shampoo bowls, though. Brenda got them imported from Italy a few years ago. They have this special neck rest that's supposed to be super ergonomic so people don't get that awful kink in their neck when they're getting their color washed out. You know how sometimes you go to a salon and your neck hurts for like two days after? Yeah, Brenda hated that, so she spent a fortune on these fancy Italian ones. They're black ceramic and they look really sleek. She even bought special microfiber towels to go with them because she read online that regular cotton towels cause too much friction and frizz on wet hair. She's so particular about that kind of stuff. But yeah... it's just this woman's voice telling me I'm ruining things, even when no one is there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that woman’s voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. Actually, yeah. Before the depression really took over, like, before I started feeling so heavy all the time, there was a stretch of several weeks where I was still getting up and functioning pretty normally. I was going to work, doing my hours, but I was already hearing her voice. And I was also just... really sure that someone at the salon was messing with my schedule. Like, deleting my appointments or moving them around in the computer. We use this booking software called Vagaro, and it's supposed to be really secure, but honestly, anyone who knows Brenda's iPad password can get in. And Brenda's password is just her dog's name and her birth year. Her dog is this tiny little Pomeranian named Muffin. Muffin is so cute, she brings him into the salon sometimes and he just sits in this little pink velvet bed under the reception desk. He has this sparkly collar with a little silver bone on it. Sometimes clients bring him treats, but Brenda is really strict about what he eats because he has a sensitive stomach. She buys this special grain-free salmon kibble that costs like eighty dollars a bag. It smells terrible, honestly, like fish food, but Muffin loves it. But um... yeah, so I was hearing the voice and feeling weird about my schedule even before I got so sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Talking less? Yeah... yeah, I definitely have been. I used to be really chatty, you know? Like, I'd always be talking with my clients, asking about their kids or their vacations, and I used to help the newer stylists a lot when they were trying to figure out color formulations. And I used to go hiking with my friends all the time on the weekends. There's this really great trail up near the reservoir that we'd always go to. It's so pretty in the fall when the leaves change. We'd always pack these little picnic lunches. My friend Sarah makes the best pasta salad for those trips. She uses those little tri-color rotini noodles and puts in fresh mozzarella balls and cherry tomatoes from her mom's garden. Her mom has this huge garden in her backyard, she grows everything. Zucchini, bell peppers, even eggplants. Last summer she had so many tomatoes she started canning her own marinara sauce and giving it out in these cute little mason jars with gingham ribbons tied around the top. I still have one sitting in my pantry actually. But um... what was I saying? Oh, right. Talking less. Yeah, now I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me anymore to be social.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that someone was messing with your schedule been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nPatient: Um, six months? No, it's been longer than that. The voices and the feeling that someone is targeting my bookings have been happening for about a year and four months now. It started right around the time Brenda decided to repaint the salon. She chose this color called \"Seafoam Whisper\" or something like that. It's this really pale, minty green. Honestly, it looks a little bit like toothpaste, but she was so excited about it. She hired these painters who came in over the weekend, and they covered everything in plastic tarps. I remember because one of the tarps got stuck to my favorite styling chair and left this weird sticky residue on the armrest. I had to use rubbing alcohol to get it off, and it took forever. Rubbing alcohol smells so strong, it always gives me a bit of a headache if I use too much of it. My mom used to use it all the time when I was a kid to clean my scraped knees when I fell off my bike. I had this bright pink bicycle with streamers on the handlebars. I loved that bike. But yeah, the voice and the schedule stuff... it's been about a year and four months.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your facial expressions feel reduced or that other people say your face looks blank or less expressive?\n\nPatient: My face? Um... yeah, I guess so. It kind of goes back to what I was just saying a minute ago, you know? About how I used to chat with all my clients and help the newer stylists and go hiking, but now I just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me to even fake a smile or react to things anymore. \n\nBrenda actually asked me if I was mad at her the other day because I was just staring blankly at the mirror while I was sweeping up hair. The mirrors in our salon are huge, they take up literally the entire wall. We have to clean them with this special glass cleaner that comes in an aerosol foam spray, otherwise they get streaks. I hate streaks on mirrors. It reminds me of when I tried to clean my car windows with regular paper towels and it just left little white fuzzies everywhere. My car is this older Honda Civic, and the AC barely works, so I have to roll the windows down in the summer. The wind always messes up my hair, which is so annoying when you work at a salon and you're supposed to look put together. We're supposed to wear all black, too. It's supposed to look chic, but it gets so hot in the summer, especially with the blow dryers running all day. \n\nBut um... yeah. Like I said earlier, I just don't have the energy to be social or expressive, so my face just stays pretty flat.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that your speech has become hard to follow or that you jump from one topic to another in a way that doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Hard to follow? Um, no, I don't think so. Nobody's ever said that to me. Usually, it's the clients who talk a mile a minute, honestly. Like my client Mrs. Gable, she comes in every three weeks for a root touch-up. We use this really specific auburn color on her, and she just talks the entire time about her grandson's baseball games. He plays in this travel league, and they have to drive all over the state for tournaments every single weekend. I don't know how people do that, driving hours and hours on the highway. I hate driving long distances. My car, like I was saying earlier, the AC is totally busted, and the radio antenna is bent so it only picks up country stations for some reason. I don't even like country music. I prefer pop, or maybe some indie bands. I went to this indie concert last year at that little venue downtown, and it was so incredibly crowded. The whole place smelled like stale beer and cheap perfume. I really can't stand strong perfumes, they always give me a headache right behind my eyes... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, jumping from topics. No, no one has mentioned anything like that about me.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt people were messing with your schedule while you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Um, a period without feeling depressed? Yeah, actually. Before the depression really took over and I just felt completely drained, there was a stretch of several weeks where I wasn't feeling down at all. I was still getting up, going to work, and functioning pretty normally, but I was already hearing that voice and feeling absolutely sure that someone at the salon was messing with my schedule. I remember because I was still trying to keep up with my regular morning routine back then. I used to make myself a smoothie every single morning. I had this really nice blender that I got on sale at Target. It was one of those Ninja ones, you know, with the multiple blades stacked up the middle? It blended ice so perfectly. I used to put frozen strawberries, a banana, and a handful of spinach in there. You can't even taste the spinach, which is crazy because it turns the whole thing this murky green color. Kind of like that \"Seafoam Whisper\" paint Brenda used on the salon walls, actually. It's funny how colors can remind you of things. Black is supposed to be slimming, but it just shows every single piece of lint and stray hair. I have this lint roller in my station drawer that I must use ten times a day. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it does say things like that. Sometimes when I'm sweeping up hair around the chairs or rinsing out the color bowls in the back, I hear this woman's voice over near the shampoo stations. She just keeps telling me that I'm ruining everything. And it happens even when the salon is totally empty or when nobody is anywhere near me. The shampoo stations are actually my least favorite part of the salon to clean. The bowls are black, so you'd think they wouldn't show stains, but the hard water leaves this weird chalky residue around the drain. We have really hard water in this town. My apartment has it too, and it completely ruins my shower head. I have to soak it in white vinegar every couple of months just to get the water to spray straight. The smell of vinegar always makes me crave salt and vinegar potato chips. I used to eat a whole bag of those while watching reality TV shows on my couch. I love the shows where people buy houses and renovate them, but they always knock down perfectly good walls to make an "open concept." I don't get why everyone wants an open concept, sometimes it's nice to have walls so you don't have to see the messy kitchen from the living room. My kitchen is so tiny, I barely have counter space for my microwave and my toaster. The toaster is one of those cheap plastic ones, and the dial is broken so it either barely warms the bread or completely burns it to a crisp... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the voice. Yeah, she tells me I'm ruining everything while I'm cleaning.

It's so unsettling. We have these really nice shampoo bowls, though. Brenda got them imported from Italy a few years ago. They have this special neck rest that's supposed to be super ergonomic so people don't get that awful kink in their neck when they're getting their color washed out. They're black ceramic and they look really sleek. She's so particular about that kind of stuff. Sometimes clients bring him treats, but Brenda is really strict about what he eats because he has a sensitive stomach. Zucchini, bell peppers, even eggplants. It started right around the time Brenda decided to repaint the salon. She chose this color called \"Seafoam Whisper\" or something like that. It's this really pale, minty green. Honestly, it looks a little bit like toothpaste, but she was so excited about it. She hired these painters who came in over the weekend, and they covered everything in plastic tarps. I remember because one of the tarps got stuck to my favorite styling chair and left this weird sticky residue on the armrest. I had to use rubbing alcohol to get it off, and it took forever. Rubbing alcohol smells so strong, it always gives me a bit of a headache if I use too much of it. My mom used to use it all the time when I was a kid to clean my scraped knees when I fell off my bike. I had this bright pink bicycle with streamers on the handlebars. I loved that bike. I hate streaks on mirrors. It reminds me of when I tried to clean my car windows with regular paper towels and it just left little white fuzzies everywhere. My car is this older Honda Civic, and the AC barely works, so I have to roll the windows down in the summer. The wind always messes up my hair, which is so annoying when you work at a salon and you're supposed to look put together. We're supposed to wear all black, too. We use this really specific auburn color on her, and she just talks the entire time about her grandson's baseball games. He plays in this travel league, and they have to drive all over the state for tournaments every single weekend. I don't know how people do that, driving hours and hours on the highway. I hate driving long distances. My car, like I was saying earlier, the AC is totally busted, and the radio antenna is bent so it only picks up country stations for some reason. I don't even like country music. I prefer pop, or maybe some indie bands. I went to this indie concert last year at that little venue downtown, and it was so incredibly crowded. The whole place smelled like stale beer and cheap perfume. I really can't stand strong perfumes, they always give me a headache right behind my eyes... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, jumping from topics. No, no one has mentioned anything like that about me.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt people were messing with your schedule while you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Um, a period without feeling depressed? I had this really nice blender that I got on sale at Target. It was one of those Ninja ones, you know, with the multiple blades stacked up the middle? It blended ice so perfectly. I used to put frozen strawberries, a banana, and a handful of spinach in there. You can't even taste the spinach, which is crazy because it turns the whole thing this murky green color. Kind of like that \"Seafoam Whisper\" paint Brenda used on the salon walls, actually. It's funny how colors can remind you of things. My favorite color used to be yellow, like sunshine yellow, but I don't really wear it anymore because it clashes with the all-black dress code at work. I still have one sitting in my pantry actually. But um... what was I saying? Oh, right. Talking less. Yeah, now I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me anymore to be social.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that someone was messing with your schedule been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nPatient: Um, six months? No, it's been longer than that. The voices and the feeling that someone is targeting my bookings have been happening for about a year and four months now. It started right around the time Brenda decided to repaint the salon. She chose this color called \"Seafoam Whisper\" or something like that. It's this really pale, minty green. Honestly, it looks a little bit like toothpaste, but she was so excited about it. She hired these painters who came in over the weekend, and they covered everything in plastic tarps. I remember because one of the tarps got stuck to my favorite styling chair and left this weird sticky residue on the armrest. I had to use rubbing alcohol to get it off, and it took forever. Rubbing alcohol smells so strong, it always gives me a bit of a headache if I use too much of it. My mom used to use it all the time when I was a kid to clean my scraped knees when I fell off my bike. I had this bright pink bicycle with streamers on the handlebars. I loved that bike. But yeah, the voice and the schedule stuff... it's been about a year and four months.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your facial expressions feel reduced or that other people say your face looks blank or less expressive?\n\nPatient: My face? Um... yeah, I guess so. It kind of goes back to what I was just saying a minute ago, you know? About how I used to chat with all my clients and help the newer stylists and go hiking, but now I just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me to even fake a smile or react to things anymore. \n\nBrenda actually asked me if I was mad at her the other day because I was just staring blankly at the mirror while I was sweeping up hair. The mirrors in our salon are huge, they take up literally the entire wall. We have to clean them with this special glass cleaner that comes in an aerosol foam spray, otherwise they get streaks. I hate streaks on mirrors. It reminds me of when I tried to clean my car windows with regular paper towels and it just left little white fuzzies everywhere. My car is this older Honda Civic, and the AC barely works, so I have to roll the windows down in the summer. The wind always messes up my hair, which is so annoying when you work at a salon and you're supposed to look put together. We're supposed to wear all black, too. It's supposed to look chic, but it gets so hot in the summer, especially with the blow dryers running all day. \n\nBut um... yeah. Like I said earlier, I just don't have the energy to be social or expressive, so my face just stays pretty flat.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that your speech has become hard to follow or that you jump from one topic to another in a way that doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Hard to follow? Um, no, I don't think so. Nobody's ever said that to me. Usually, it's the clients who talk a mile a minute, honestly. Like my client Mrs. Gable, she comes in every three weeks for a root touch-up. We use this really specific auburn color on her, and she just talks the entire time about her grandson's baseball games. He plays in this travel league, and they have to drive all over the state for tournaments every single weekend. I don't know how people do that, driving hours and hours on the highway. I hate driving long distances. My car, like I was saying earlier, the AC is totally busted, and the radio antenna is bent so it only picks up country stations for some reason. I don't even like country music. I prefer pop, or maybe some indie bands. I went to this indie concert last year at that little venue downtown, and it was so incredibly crowded. The whole place smelled like stale beer and cheap perfume. I really can't stand strong perfumes, they always give me a headache right behind my eyes... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, jumping from topics. No, no one has mentioned anything like that about me.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt people were messing with your schedule while you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Um, a period without feeling depressed? Yeah, actually. Before the depression really took over and I just felt completely drained, there was a stretch of several weeks where I wasn't feeling down at all. I was still getting up, going to work, and functioning pretty normally, but I was already hearing that voice and feeling absolutely sure that someone at the salon was messing with my schedule. I remember because I was still trying to keep up with my regular morning routine back then. I used to make myself a smoothie every single morning. I had this really nice blender that I got on sale at Target. It was one of those Ninja ones, you know, with the multiple blades stacked up the middle? It blended ice so perfectly. I used to put frozen strawberries, a banana, and a handful of spinach in there. You can't even taste the spinach, which is crazy because it turns the whole thing this murky green color. Kind of like that \"Seafoam Whisper\" paint Brenda used on the salon walls, actually. It's funny how colors can remind you of things. My favorite color used to be yellow, like sunshine yellow, but I don't really wear it anymore because it clashes with the all-black dress code at work. Black is supposed to be slimming, but it just shows every single piece of lint and stray hair. I have this lint roller in my station drawer that I must use ten times a day. The sticky sheets always tear weirdly when you try to peel them off, though... But yeah, there were definitely a few weeks where I wasn't depressed yet, but the voice and the schedule stuff were already happening.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the woman’s voice, does it specifically say negative things about you like that you’re worthless or have done something terrible?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does say things like that. Sometimes when I'm sweeping up hair around the chairs or rinsing out the color bowls in the back, I hear this woman's voice over near the shampoo stations. She just keeps telling me that I'm ruining everything. And it happens even when the salon is totally empty or when nobody is anywhere near me. The shampoo stations are actually my least favorite part of the salon to clean. The bowls are black, so you'd think they wouldn't show stains, but the hard water leaves this weird chalky residue around the drain. We have really hard water in this town. My apartment has it too, and it completely ruins my shower head. I have to soak it in white vinegar every couple of months just to get the water to spray straight. The smell of vinegar always makes me crave salt and vinegar potato chips. I used to eat a whole bag of those while watching reality TV shows on my couch. I love the shows where people buy houses and renovate them, but they always knock down perfectly good walls to make an \"open concept.\" I don't get why everyone wants an open concept, sometimes it's nice to have walls so you don't have to see the messy kitchen from the living room. My kitchen is so tiny, I barely have counter space for my microwave and my toaster. The toaster is one of those cheap plastic ones, and the dial is broken so it either barely warms the bread or completely burns it to a crisp... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the voice. Yeah, she tells me I'm ruining everything while I'm cleaning.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, emotionally flat? Yeah, I guess so. Even when I wasn't feeling totally bogged down by the depression, I still wasn't really... me, you know? Like, I used to chat with all my clients, help the newer stylists mix their colors, and on weekends I'd go hiking with my friends. We used to go up to this state park about an hour from here. The trails there are gorgeous, especially in the fall when the leaves change. I love autumn, it's my favorite season. The air just smells so crisp, and you can finally wear sweaters again. I have this huge collection of oversized knit sweaters that I keep in a cedar chest at the end of my bed. The cedar smells so good, it reminds me of my grandfather's workshop. He used to build birdhouses out of cedar and pine. He had this massive table saw in his garage that always terrified me when I was little. It was so loud, and there was sawdust everywhere. I hate sawdust, it gets in your eyes and makes you sneeze. Kind of like the hairspray we use at the salon. Brenda buys this industrial-strength aerosol stuff that literally leaves a sticky cloud in the air. If you walk through it, you can feel it coating your eyelashes. I have to use waterproof mascara just so it doesn't run when I try to wipe the hairspray off my face. Waterproof mascara is a nightmare to take off at night, though. You need that special oil-based makeup remover, and it always gets in my eyes and makes my vision blurry for like ten minutes... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, not talking much. Yeah, now I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me anymore, even when I'm not feeling super depressed.

I'm a hairstylist, and I love doing color, especially balayage—it's like painting, you know? I used to could do a full head of foils in like forty-five minutes. My instructor in cosmetology school said I had the fastest hands she'd ever seen. She had this really loud laugh, like a honk almost... anyway, lately, my hands just feel like they’re moving through glue. Even just sectioning the hair or mixing the color in the bowl, it takes me forever now. My manager pulled me aside yesterday—she wears these huge hoop earrings that clink when she talks—and she said people have started noticing I’m just way slower than I used to be. I don't know why I can't just move normally anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a tremor in your hands when they’re resting?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed them shaking or anything when I'm just sitting there. It's more just this heavy feeling, like they're weighed down. Which is weird because I used to have such steady hands. You need really steady hands to do eyeliner too, which I used to love doing before going out. My friend Jess and I would spend hours getting ready, trying all these different makeup tutorials we'd find online. She's the one who actually told me I should go to cosmetology school. We were sitting in her bedroom—she had this bright pink comforter that I always told her was way too loud—and she just looked at me and said, \"You're too good at this to not do it for a living.\" I miss hanging out with her. We haven't really talked much lately. I don't know, it's just... no, no shaking. Just the slowness.\n\nDoctor: Have your muscles been feeling stiff?\n\nPatient: Stiff? Um... no, not really. They don't feel tight or anything. I don't feel like I need to stretch them out. I used to stretch all the time when I was taking this Pilates class, though. My mom actually bought me a package of classes for my birthday last year because she said I needed to work on my posture since I'm always hunched over the shampoo bowl at work. The instructor there, her name was Kenzie, she always wore these neon green leggings that were so bright they kind of hurt your eyes to look at in the studio mirrors. She made us do all these weird core exercises on those sliding machine things, the reformers? It was so hard, I was sore for like a week straight after the first time. But um... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. No, my muscles aren't stiff right now. Just really heavy and slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you started taking an antidepressant recently?\n\nPatient: Antidepressant? Um, no. I'm not taking anything like that. I don't really like taking pills anyway. Even when I get a headache, I try to just drink water or something. My grandma used to have this whole cabinet full of orange pill bottles. It took up an entire shelf in her kitchen, right next to where she kept her fancy teacups. She collected teacups from all over the world, even though she never left the state. She had this one from London with a little gold rim that she said was her favorite, but she never actually drank out of it. She just liked looking at it. I think she had a pill for almost everything, though... blood pressure, cholesterol, vitamins. But no, I haven't started taking any new medications or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I have. Most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest. It just sits there, making me feel like there's no point in trying to build the life I was planning, you know? Like, what's the point of finishing my hours for my license? Which sucks because I've already put in so much time at the salon. I started sweeping hair there when I was 16. My boss, Brenda, she's been cutting hair since the 80s and she always tells these crazy stories about the big perms she used to do. She said they used so much hairspray back then that the floor would literally be sticky by the end of the day. She has this framed photo of herself from 1987 with hair that's teased so high it barely fits in the picture. It's actually really funny. We used to laugh about it a lot in the breakroom. But um... yeah, I just feel really sad and flat lately. It's hard to care about any of that stuff right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually high or energized?\n\nPatient: High or energized? Um... no. I don't think so. I've never really felt like that. I'm usually pretty low-key, honestly. Even when I was a kid, my mom said I was always the quiet one at birthday parties. Speaking of birthday parties, my cousin just had a huge one for her 21st last weekend. She rented out this whole rooftop bar downtown, the one with the string lights and the little fire pits? It looked so pretty in the pictures she posted on Instagram. She wore this sparkly silver dress that she ordered online, but she had to get it altered because it was way too long. The seamstress she went to actually works in the same strip mall as my salon. Her name is Maria and she always brings us these amazing homemade tamales around the holidays. They're so good, especially the green chile ones. She wraps them in foil and keeps them in this little cooler so they stay warm. But um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right. Energized. No, I've never really had anything like that. Just the heavy, sad feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices that other people can’t hear?\n\nPatient: Voices? Um... yeah, actually. It's kind of weird. Sometimes when I'm at work, like when I'm sweeping up hair or rinsing out the color bowls, I'll hear this woman's voice. She's usually over by the shampoo stations, and she just keeps saying that I'm ruining everything. It happens even when the salon is completely empty or nobody is anywhere near me. It's so unsettling. We have these really nice shampoo bowls, though. Brenda got them imported from Italy a few years ago. They have this special neck rest that's supposed to be super ergonomic so people don't get that awful kink in their neck when they're getting their color washed out. You know how sometimes you go to a salon and your neck hurts for like two days after? Yeah, Brenda hated that, so she spent a fortune on these fancy Italian ones. They're black ceramic and they look really sleek. She even bought special microfiber towels to go with them because she read online that regular cotton towels cause too much friction and frizz on wet hair. She's so particular about that kind of stuff. But yeah... it's just this woman's voice telling me I'm ruining things, even when no one is there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that woman’s voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. Actually, yeah. Before the depression really took over, like, before I started feeling so heavy all the time, there was a stretch of several weeks where I was still getting up and functioning pretty normally. I was going to work, doing my hours, but I was already hearing her voice. And I was also just... really sure that someone at the salon was messing with my schedule. Like, deleting my appointments or moving them around in the computer. We use this booking software called Vagaro, and it's supposed to be really secure, but honestly, anyone who knows Brenda's iPad password can get in. And Brenda's password is just her dog's name and her birth year. Her dog is this tiny little Pomeranian named Muffin. Muffin is so cute, she brings him into the salon sometimes and he just sits in this little pink velvet bed under the reception desk. He has this sparkly collar with a little silver bone on it. Sometimes clients bring him treats, but Brenda is really strict about what he eats because he has a sensitive stomach. She buys this special grain-free salmon kibble that costs like eighty dollars a bag. It smells terrible, honestly, like fish food, but Muffin loves it. But um... yeah, so I was hearing the voice and feeling weird about my schedule even before I got so sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Talking less? Yeah... yeah, I definitely have been. I used to be really chatty, you know? Like, I'd always be talking with my clients, asking about their kids or their vacations, and I used to help the newer stylists a lot when they were trying to figure out color formulations. And I used to go hiking with my friends all the time on the weekends. There's this really great trail up near the reservoir that we'd always go to. It's so pretty in the fall when the leaves change. We'd always pack these little picnic lunches. My friend Sarah makes the best pasta salad for those trips. She uses those little tri-color rotini noodles and puts in fresh mozzarella balls and cherry tomatoes from her mom's garden. Her mom has this huge garden in her backyard, she grows everything. Zucchini, bell peppers, even eggplants. Last summer she had so many tomatoes she started canning her own marinara sauce and giving it out in these cute little mason jars with gingham ribbons tied around the top. I still have one sitting in my pantry actually. But um... what was I saying? Oh, right. Talking less. Yeah, now I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me anymore to be social.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that someone was messing with your schedule been going on for 6 months or longer?\n\nPatient: Um, six months? No, it's been longer than that. The voices and the feeling that someone is targeting my bookings have been happening for about a year and four months now. It started right around the time Brenda decided to repaint the salon. She chose this color called \"Seafoam Whisper\" or something like that. It's this really pale, minty green. Honestly, it looks a little bit like toothpaste, but she was so excited about it. She hired these painters who came in over the weekend, and they covered everything in plastic tarps. I remember because one of the tarps got stuck to my favorite styling chair and left this weird sticky residue on the armrest. I had to use rubbing alcohol to get it off, and it took forever. Rubbing alcohol smells so strong, it always gives me a bit of a headache if I use too much of it. My mom used to use it all the time when I was a kid to clean my scraped knees when I fell off my bike. I had this bright pink bicycle with streamers on the handlebars. I loved that bike. But yeah, the voice and the schedule stuff... it's been about a year and four months.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your facial expressions feel reduced or that other people say your face looks blank or less expressive?\n\nPatient: My face? Um... yeah, I guess so. It kind of goes back to what I was just saying a minute ago, you know? About how I used to chat with all my clients and help the newer stylists and go hiking, but now I just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me to even fake a smile or react to things anymore. \n\nBrenda actually asked me if I was mad at her the other day because I was just staring blankly at the mirror while I was sweeping up hair. The mirrors in our salon are huge, they take up literally the entire wall. We have to clean them with this special glass cleaner that comes in an aerosol foam spray, otherwise they get streaks. I hate streaks on mirrors. It reminds me of when I tried to clean my car windows with regular paper towels and it just left little white fuzzies everywhere. My car is this older Honda Civic, and the AC barely works, so I have to roll the windows down in the summer. The wind always messes up my hair, which is so annoying when you work at a salon and you're supposed to look put together. We're supposed to wear all black, too. It's supposed to look chic, but it gets so hot in the summer, especially with the blow dryers running all day. \n\nBut um... yeah. Like I said earlier, I just don't have the energy to be social or expressive, so my face just stays pretty flat.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that your speech has become hard to follow or that you jump from one topic to another in a way that doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Hard to follow? Um, no, I don't think so. Nobody's ever said that to me. Usually, it's the clients who talk a mile a minute, honestly. Like my client Mrs. Gable, she comes in every three weeks for a root touch-up. We use this really specific auburn color on her, and she just talks the entire time about her grandson's baseball games. He plays in this travel league, and they have to drive all over the state for tournaments every single weekend. I don't know how people do that, driving hours and hours on the highway. I hate driving long distances. My car, like I was saying earlier, the AC is totally busted, and the radio antenna is bent so it only picks up country stations for some reason. I don't even like country music. I prefer pop, or maybe some indie bands. I went to this indie concert last year at that little venue downtown, and it was so incredibly crowded. The whole place smelled like stale beer and cheap perfume. I really can't stand strong perfumes, they always give me a headache right behind my eyes... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, jumping from topics. No, no one has mentioned anything like that about me.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt people were messing with your schedule while you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Um, a period without feeling depressed? Yeah, actually. Before the depression really took over and I just felt completely drained, there was a stretch of several weeks where I wasn't feeling down at all. I was still getting up, going to work, and functioning pretty normally, but I was already hearing that voice and feeling absolutely sure that someone at the salon was messing with my schedule. I remember because I was still trying to keep up with my regular morning routine back then. I used to make myself a smoothie every single morning. I had this really nice blender that I got on sale at Target. It was one of those Ninja ones, you know, with the multiple blades stacked up the middle? It blended ice so perfectly. I used to put frozen strawberries, a banana, and a handful of spinach in there. You can't even taste the spinach, which is crazy because it turns the whole thing this murky green color. Kind of like that \"Seafoam Whisper\" paint Brenda used on the salon walls, actually. It's funny how colors can remind you of things. My favorite color used to be yellow, like sunshine yellow, but I don't really wear it anymore because it clashes with the all-black dress code at work. Black is supposed to be slimming, but it just shows every single piece of lint and stray hair. I have this lint roller in my station drawer that I must use ten times a day. The sticky sheets always tear weirdly when you try to peel them off, though... But yeah, there were definitely a few weeks where I wasn't depressed yet, but the voice and the schedule stuff were already happening.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the woman’s voice, does it specifically say negative things about you like that you’re worthless or have done something terrible?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does say things like that. Sometimes when I'm sweeping up hair around the chairs or rinsing out the color bowls in the back, I hear this woman's voice over near the shampoo stations. She just keeps telling me that I'm ruining everything. And it happens even when the salon is totally empty or when nobody is anywhere near me. The shampoo stations are actually my least favorite part of the salon to clean. The bowls are black, so you'd think they wouldn't show stains, but the hard water leaves this weird chalky residue around the drain. We have really hard water in this town. My apartment has it too, and it completely ruins my shower head. I have to soak it in white vinegar every couple of months just to get the water to spray straight. The smell of vinegar always makes me crave salt and vinegar potato chips. I used to eat a whole bag of those while watching reality TV shows on my couch. I love the shows where people buy houses and renovate them, but they always knock down perfectly good walls to make an \"open concept.\" I don't get why everyone wants an open concept, sometimes it's nice to have walls so you don't have to see the messy kitchen from the living room. My kitchen is so tiny, I barely have counter space for my microwave and my toaster. The toaster is one of those cheap plastic ones, and the dial is broken so it either barely warms the bread or completely burns it to a crisp... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the voice. Yeah, she tells me I'm ruining everything while I'm cleaning.\n\nDoctor: During times when you were not feeling depressed, did you still find yourself not talking much or seeming emotionally flat?\n\nPatient: Um, emotionally flat? Yeah, I guess so. Even when I wasn't feeling totally bogged down by the depression, I still wasn't really... me, you know? Like, I used to chat with all my clients, help the newer stylists mix their colors, and on weekends I'd go hiking with my friends. We used to go up to this state park about an hour from here. The trails there are gorgeous, especially in the fall when the leaves change. I love autumn, it's my favorite season. The air just smells so crisp, and you can finally wear sweaters again. I have this huge collection of oversized knit sweaters that I keep in a cedar chest at the end of my bed. The cedar smells so good, it reminds me of my grandfather's workshop. He used to build birdhouses out of cedar and pine. He had this massive table saw in his garage that always terrified me when I was little. It was so loud, and there was sawdust everywhere. I hate sawdust, it gets in your eyes and makes you sneeze. Kind of like the hairspray we use at the salon. Brenda buys this industrial-strength aerosol stuff that literally leaves a sticky cloud in the air. If you walk through it, you can feel it coating your eyelashes. I have to use waterproof mascara just so it doesn't run when I try to wipe the hairspray off my face. Waterproof mascara is a nightmare to take off at night, though. You need that special oil-based makeup remover, and it always gets in my eyes and makes my vision blurry for like ten minutes... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, not talking much. Yeah, now I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me anymore, even when I'm not feeling super depressed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I remember because I was still trying to keep up with my regular morning routine back then. I used to make myself a smoothie every single morning. I had this really nice blender that I got on sale at Target. It was one of those Ninja ones, you know, with the multiple blades stacked up the middle? It blended ice so perfectly. I used to put frozen strawberries, a banana, and a handful of spinach in there. You can't even taste the spinach, which is crazy because it turns the whole thing this murky green color. Kind of like that \"Seafoam Whisper\" paint Brenda used on the salon walls, actually. It's funny how colors can remind you of things. My favorite color used to be yellow, like sunshine yellow, but I don't really wear it anymore because it clashes with the all-black dress code at work. Black is supposed to be slimming, but it just shows every single piece of lint and stray hair. I have this lint roller in my station drawer that I must use ten times a day. The sticky sheets always tear weirdly when you try to peel them off, though... She just keeps telling me that I'm ruining everything. And it happens even when the salon is totally empty or when nobody is anywhere near me. The shampoo stations are actually my least favorite part of the salon to clean. The bowls are black, so you'd think they wouldn't show stains, but the hard water leaves this weird chalky residue around the drain. We have really hard water in this town. My apartment has it too, and it completely ruins my shower head. I have to soak it in white vinegar every couple of months just to get the water to spray straight. The smell of vinegar always makes me crave salt and vinegar potato chips. I used to eat a whole bag of those while watching reality TV shows on my couch. I love the shows where people buy houses and renovate them, but they always knock down perfectly good walls to make an \"open concept.\" I don't get why everyone wants an open concept, sometimes it's nice to have walls so you don't have to see the messy kitchen from the living room. My kitchen is so tiny, I barely have counter space for my microwave and my toaster. The toaster is one of those cheap plastic ones, and the dial is broken so it either barely warms the bread or completely burns it to a crisp... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the voice. Yeah, she tells me I'm ruining everything while I'm cleaning.\n\nDoctor: During times when you were not feeling depressed, did you still find yourself not talking much or seeming emotionally flat?\n\nPatient: Um, emotionally flat? I have this huge collection of oversized knit sweaters that I keep in a cedar chest at the end of my bed. The cedar smells so good, it reminds me of my grandfather's workshop. He used to build birdhouses out of cedar and pine. He had this massive table saw in his garage that always terrified me when I was little. It was so loud, and there was sawdust everywhere. I hate sawdust, it gets in your eyes and makes you sneeze. Kind of like the hairspray we use at the salon. Brenda buys this industrial-strength aerosol stuff that literally leaves a sticky cloud in the air. If you walk through it, you can feel it coating your eyelashes. I have to use waterproof mascara just so it doesn't run when I try to wipe the hairspray off my face. Waterproof mascara is a nightmare to take off at night, though. You need that special oil-based makeup remover, and it always gets in my eyes and makes my vision blurry for like ten minutes... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, not talking much. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, slowed down? Yeah, actually, a few people have mentioned it. It's weird because my hands feel like they're moving through glue at work. Like, even just sectioning hair or mixing color takes me forever now, and people have started noticing that I'm just way slower than I used to be. Mixing color is usually my favorite part of the job, honestly. You have to be so precise with the ratios, like a little chemist. We use these little digital scales that measure in grams. Mine has a crack across the screen from when I dropped a bottle of developer on it. Developer is so annoying to clean up, it's thick and creamy and it kind of bleaches whatever it sits on for too long. I ruined my favorite pair of jeans that way. They were these perfectly distressed Levi's that I found at a thrift store for like ten bucks. I love thrifting, you can find the best stuff if you have the patience to dig through the racks. The thrift store near my apartment always smells like old books and mothballs, but in a comforting way. My grandma's house used to smell like mothballs. She had this huge attic full of old clothes and weird antique furniture. She had this one velvet chair that was bright pink, like, aggressively pink. I always wanted to take it for my apartment, but it wouldn't fit in my tiny car. I drive a Honda Civic, and the trunk is practically nonexistent. Plus, the AC is broken, so in the summer I just have to roll all the windows down and let the wind mess up my hair... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, moving slow. Yeah, my hands just feel like they're stuck in glue, and it takes me forever to do anything at the salon now.
